+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49302	(@Sharadhi-v98, #49302)	net: Account for a trailing dot in public suffix lookups (#49302)
    security; fixed site checks for host names with a trailing dot
    ^ commit c5940fae15948c81916b24e533703d5e354ab42f
    # The public suffix list lookup in `PubDomainRules` is done on the raw host name. The URL parser keeps a trailing dot in a host, so for `attacker.com.` the candidate suffixes are `com.` and the empty string, neither of which is on the list. `reg_suffix` then returns `com.` for every `*.com.` host and `is_pub_domain("com.")` is false. `is_same_site`, the `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-site` check and the cookie `Domain` check all build on these helpers, so a page on `https://attacker.com./` is treated as same-site with `https://victim.com./`: its subresource loads pass a `same-site` CORP check, its requests carry `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-site`, and `Set-Cookie: a=b; Domain=com.` is accepted. The URL spec runs the list algorithm without the trailing dot and then puts it back on the result, so do the same here.
    # 
    # Testing: Added `test_trailing_dot` to `components/shared/net/tests/pub_domains.rs`, which fails without this change (`cargo test -p servo-net-traits --test pub_domains`). `cargo test -p servo-net` also passes.
    # 
+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49417	(@yezhizhen, #49417)	script: Implement readEntries() state machine + Chromium style batching (#49417)
    dom wip; folder uploads with <input type=file webkitdirectory> and the File and Directory Entries API (behind dom_entries_api_enabled)
    ^ commit e75670c3186f5c6b9fdff4f39762847830e9a97b
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # 1. This implements state machine for [readEntries](https://wicg.github.io/entries-api/#dom-filesystemdirectoryentry-readentries): the motivation is to 
    # - fail consecutive `readEntries` calls before the previous async callback is run
    # - Run error callback when reading fails
    # 
    # 2. This adds 100 batching size like Chromium. It was baffling for me why spec here was "unnecessarily complicated", until I found this on MDN:
    # > In Chromium-based browsers, readEntries() will only return the first 100 FileSystemEntry instances. In order to obtain all of the instances, readEntries() must be called multiple times.
    # 
    # Testing: This adds an automated tests, with helpers to reduce duplication from previous two exported tests.
    # Part of #45653
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49268	(@veyndan, #49268)	android: Replace `ServoView` with `AndroidExternalSurface` (#49268)
    api; Android: ServoView replaced with AndroidExternalSurface, back handling and soft keyboard moved into the library, and API cleanups
    ^ commit 2d31b933f6cd13432c5a5a76bb2e521331757b16
    # Instead of having our esoteric setup of an `AndroidView` and a custom `ServoView` containing a `Surface`, we can instead just use the built-in `AndroidExternalSurface`. This essentially does what we're currently doing under-the-hood, but it has some nice helper methods and is importantly shorter and more "standardised" than our current approach.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49292	(@veyndan, #49292)	android: Preserve history title nullability (#49292)
    api; Android: ServoView replaced with AndroidExternalSurface, back handling and soft keyboard moved into the library, and API cleanups
    ^ commit 56aef4b1a45a19534caaddfc8dd3191a1351f921
    # When a `null` title goes in to `HistoryEntry`, an empty string is serialised and deserialised. To preserve the nullability of the title, let's just serialise and deserialise the title as-is.
    # 
    # This is minor and doesn't change anything in practice. What it does do though is unlock the usage of KotlinX Serialization to generate the serialisation and deserialisation logic for us, as it'd generate something similar to how it's now written.
    # 
    # Testing: There are no automated tests for Android.
+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49152	(@Daniel-1600, #49152)	devtools: Add mimeType and sizes to responses (#49152)
    devtools; the Network panel now shows the MIME type and size of responses, and large response bodies are truncated
    ^ commit b67f66c2de48eb9b6c25a57976551db6f2cb8c98
    # Firefox's Network panel showed an empty Type and Size column and a "0 B" total for requests from Servo, because `ResponseFields` never sent `mimeType`, `transferredSize` or `contentSize`. This adds the three fields and fills them in `response_fields()` the same way `getResponseContent` does: `mimeType` from the `Content-Type` header, `transferredSize` from `Content-Length`, and `contentSize` from the response body length (0 if there is no body). A missing header leaves its field out instead of sending `null`.
    # 
    # Testing: No new automated tests. The existing tests work as before 
    # before

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49473	(@veyndan, #49473)	android: Remove `:servoview-local` (#49473)
    servoshell; Android: modernised Gradle build and many cleanups
    ^ commit 24273eab73c91477fd60e09eaf92c3e28a7ad199
    # Removes  `:servoview-local`  as it's:
    # 
    # 1. Completely undocumented.
    # 2. Needless, since the actual building of  `:servoview`  is very quick compared to all the other builds.
    # 3. It's non-standard, as one would usually just enable Gradle build caching for such a thing. The project is small/quick enough that there hasn't been a strong need to enable this so far.
    # 4. Most likely frustrating to work with, since one can only drop in an AAR if the API of the compilation is compatible with the current incarnation of  `:servoview`. Otherwise, annoying incompatibility errors will ensue.
    # 5. It adds a bit of file IO during the Gradle build, something that will marginally slow down every build regardless of whether  `:servoview-local`  is used or not.
    # 
    # Testing: There are no automated tests for Android.
    #

    # Adds support for console.table, rendering tabular data as a text-based table sent to the embedder, with raw data forwarded to devtools under a new ConsoleLogLevel::Table. Falls back to a plain log call when the argument can't be parsed as tabular data.
    # 
    # Mirrors Firefox's console-messages.js handling of `level === "table"`: only the first two arguments (data, columns) are forwarded, with the data's `ownProperties`/`preview` rebuilt from its grip so the devtools frontend can render the table. Falls back to `ConsoleLogLevel::Log` when the argument can't be previewed as an object.
    # 
    # Testing:  Covers arrays, objects, Maps, nested rows, the columns argument, argument truncation, and the fallback to `ConsoleLogLevel::Log` for non-tabular data, matching Firefox's `console-messages.js` behavior.
    # 
    # Fixes: #27212

    #   of completed.
    # - Proper handling of stopped loads at various times during the loading

    # Bumps [resvg](https://github.com/linebender/resvg) from 0.48.1 to 0.49.0.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/linebender/resvg/releases">resvg's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>v0.49.0</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>resvg-0.*.0.tar.xz</code> is a sources archive with vendored Rust dependencies</li>
    # <li><code>resvg-explorer-extension.exe</code> is an SVG thumbnailer for Windows Explorer</li>
    # </ul>
    # <p>Check <a href="https://github.com/linebender/resvg/blob/refs/tags/v0.49.0/CHANGELOG.md">CHANGELOG</a>.</p>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/linebender/resvg/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md">resvg's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>[0.49.0] 2026-10-24</h2>
    # <p>This release has an MSRV of 1.85.0 for <code>usvg</code> and <code>resvg</code> and the C API.</p>
    # <h3>Added</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>--query-bbox</code> CLI option to query the drawing bounding box without rasterizing it. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1050">#1050</a>)</li>
    # <li><code>mix-blend-mode: plus-lighter</code> (Compositing and Blending Level 2). (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1142">#1142</a>)</li>
    # <li><code>feComposite</code> <code>operator=&quot;lighter&quot;</code> (SVG 2). (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1142">#1142</a>)</li>
    # <li>Prebuilt <code>aarch64</code> release binaries for Linux and Windows. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1132">#1132</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Changed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>usvg::BlendMode</code> and <code>usvg::filter::CompositeOperator</code> have new <code>PlusLighter</code>
    # and <code>Lighter</code> variants. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1142">#1142</a>)</li>
    # <li><code>Group::layer_bounding_box</code> of a group without filters is now clipped by its clip path,
    # so clipped groups allocate smaller layers. This also affects the sizes reported by
    # <code>--query-all</code> and used by <code>--export-id</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1158">#1158</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>dominant-baseline</code> is now correctly inherited by nested <code>&lt;tspan&gt;</code> elements,
    # so a nested span stays on the same baseline as its siblings. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1086">#1086</a>)</li>
    # <li>Panics and incorrect clipping when a filter region is smaller than its input. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1144">#1144</a>)</li>
    # <li>Incorrect transform order on the outermost <code>svg</code> element with a <code>viewBox</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1085">#1085</a>)</li>
    # <li>Relative positioning of right-to-left text with <code>dx</code> and <code>dy</code>, including text on a path and text decorations. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1145">#1145</a>)</li>
    # <li><code>text-anchor</code> now uses the actual extent of a text chunk, which <code>dx</code> and <code>textLength</code> can change. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1145">#1145</a>)</li>
    # <li>Filters inside rotated or skewed groups are now applied in the user space of the group.
    # This fixes the clipping to filter regions and subregions, as well as the direction of
    # offsets, blurs, turbulence and tiles, and the rendering of <code>feImage</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1155">#1155</a>)</li>
    # <li><code>gradientTransform</code> and <code>patternTransform</code> are now inherited via <code>href</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1135">#1135</a>)</li>
    # <li>The bounding box of an <code>&lt;image&gt;</code> with <code>preserveAspectRatio=&quot;… slice&quot;</code> is now its viewport,
    # so <code>objectBoundingBox</code> clip paths, masks and filters on it are resolved correctly. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1077">#1077</a>)</li>
    # <li>Markers are now clipped to their viewport instead of their <code>viewBox</code> when the aspect ratios differ. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1163">#1163</a>)</li>
    # <li>Object bounding box gradients inside masks, patterns and <code>feImage</code> filters used by more than one element
    # are now resolved correctly, instead of being rendered as a flat fill or not at all. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1164">#1164</a>)</li>
    # <li><code>feMorphology</code> kernels are now symmetric and fractional radii are rounded to the nearest pixel. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1120">#1120</a>)</li>
    # <li>Font fallback log messages now always name the correct fallback font family. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1154">#1154</a>)</li>
    # <li>The <code>resvg</code> CLI no longer rounds fractional image sizes before scaling, and rounds the output size up
    # instead of to the nearest integer. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/875">#875</a>)</li>
    # <li>The C API header was missing <code>RESVG_ERROR_SVGZ_UNSUPPORTED</code>, so all following error codes were off by one. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1122">#1122</a>)</li>
    # <li><code>cargo-c</code> now installs <code>ResvgQt.h</code> alongside <code>resvg.h</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/linebender/resvg/issues/1182">#1182</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49507	(@veyndan, #49507)	android: Remove explicit setting of `buildToolsVersion` (#49507)
    servoshell; Android: modernised Gradle build and many cleanups
    ^ commit 23be351afcdf5b6cea81bbeefc50cedd596239f6
    # A `buildToolsVersion` is [automatically set](https://developer.android.com/tools/releases/build-tools) via the Android Gradle Plugin (AGP). The specific version can be found on the AGP release page, for example for 9.4.1 [it's 36.0.0](https://developer.android.com/build/releases/agp-9-4-0-release-notes#compatibility).
    # 
    # Relying on the default version provided means that:
    # 
    # 1. No compatibility errors with the AGP will accidentally arise, as they are the one setting the version.
    # 2. The `buildToolsVersion` will be automatically kept up-to-date with Dependabot, as Dependabot upgrades AGP which will internally bump the `buildToolsVersion` if necessary.
    # 
    # Testing: There are no automated tests for Android.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49414	(@fran6co, #49414)	paint: tell refresh driver observers of frame starts, rather than of every paint (#49414)
    dom; animations and requestAnimationFrame now tick at a steady rate, even during user input
    ^ commit b7ba3fddf9525750d49540822af8556f30207e25
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    # http://github.com/servo/servo/pull/40433 makes every paint count as a frame start, so paints from sources like input also tick requestAnimationFrame and animations. It does create some weird behaviour of animations ticking faster when there is input from any source, but it's not super noticeable. But with an embedder driving the frames with a RefreshDriver, the animations would still tick on the driver's frame starts and other paints. This fix brings the guard back but it does queue notifying the observers to the next frame instead of doing immediately avoiding the stall that http://github.com/servo/servo/pull/40433 fixed.
    # 
    # Testing: Added 3 tests that fail in main:
    # - how many animation ticks we get with the default 120Hz refresh driver under input
    # - a paint between frames doesn't ask for another paint until the next frame starts, and does once it starts
    # - paints between frames don't advance animations
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49549	(@gabrielecabrini, #49549)	script: Use ensure_pre_insertion_validity in Node.replaceChild (#49549)
    crash; fixed crashes in replaceChild() and moveBefore() with shadow DOM
    ^ commit c223e605a6b2d2303ea40f5f6e483143a462f585
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # `ReplaceChild` had its own copy of the pre-insertion checks. That copy used `is_inclusive_ancestor_of`
    # instead of `is_host_including_inclusive_ancestor`, so a shadow host's ancestor could be inserted
    # into the host's shadow root, and `bind_to_tree` then recursed forever.
    # 
    # As the spec now does ("ensure pre-insert validity given node, parent, child, and « child »"),
    # `ReplaceChild` now calls `Node::ensure_pre_insertion_validity`, which gets a `children_to_exclude`
    # parameter. `moveBefore` gets the same host-including check fix.
    # 
    # Testing: Added a WPT crashtest
    # Fixes: #49533

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49404	(@mxmgorin, #49404)	webgl: Keep the sized internal format of texImage2D images (#49404)
    dom; many WebGL and WebGL 2 fixes, including zero-initialised texture storage and non-power-of-two mipmaps
    ^ commit 3139f3cd2e223d754362e81c556c3a5553c289cf
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # This change makes `texImage2D` store the sized `internalformat` of an image. Previously, it stored the unsized `format`, causing WebGL 2 framebuffer checks to incorrectly classify such textures as incomplete. This affected depth textures, `R8`/`RG8`, and integer formats.
    # 
    # With the actual internal format stored, these now follow [OpenGL ES 3.0.6](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf):
    # - Color-renderable formats are determined by [table 3.13](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#page=143).
    # - `readPixels` and `IMPLEMENTATION_COLOR_READ_FORMAT`/`TYPE` accept the format/type pair required by the read buffer's component type ([section 4.3.2](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#page=204)).
    # - `copyTexImage2D` validates the source and destination formats ([section 3.8.5](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#page=152)).
    # 
    # A `copyTexImage2D` image with an unsized `internalformat` now records the effective format that section 3.8.5 assigns it (a floating-point one for a floating-point color buffer, as `EXT_color_buffer_half_float` expects), so framebuffers that attach such a copy can be read back and copied from. As a side effect, WebGL 1 `texSubImage2D` after `copyTexImage2D` no longer panics on the image's missing type.
    # This also fixes `readPixels` into a pixel pack buffer, which previously used the channel count as the number of bytes per pixel.
    # Formats from `EXT_color_buffer_float` remain color-renderable for now, since Servo does not yet implement that extension; a follow-up adds it and gates them.
    # 
    # Testing: Many WebGL 2 conformance subtests now pass.
    # `depth-stencil-feedback-loop` fails because depth textures can now be attached, and Servo does not yet detect depth/stencil feedback loops.
    # `ext-color-buffer-float` gains four additional failures similar to the existing ones, affecting R/RG float textures that texImage2D now stores using sized internal formats.
    # Added WPT test, `webgl/copyTexImage2D-unsized-effective-format.html`, covers reading back and copying from unsized `copyTexImage2D` images, and `texSubImage2D` into them in WebGL 1.
-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49546	(@Gae24, #49546)	script_bindings: Clean-up `Default` implementation for dictionary types (#49546)
    ^ commit 04ddd0a6d501010214f60af4fc1d597ae07d6448
    # Converting dictionary types to use `TracedDOMString` breaks code generation for some of them. Since these dictionaries now require tracing, `Default` implementation calls `Default::default` for each field instead of relying on the `empty` method. However, `Default::default` is not implemented for union types.
    # 
    # To fix it, this PR flips the two implementations: `Default` now initializes the dictionary using the default values specified by the WebIDL, and `empty` now simply invokes `Default` and wraps it in a `RootedTraceableBox` if needed. Additionally for trivial implementations manual implementation is skipped and instead add `Default` to the derive attributes.
    # 
    # Testing: It compiles
    # Part of #48359
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49159	(@jschwe, #49159)	script_bindings: Generate binding tables as constant data (#49159)
    perf; various small speedups in layout and script
    ^ commit 0d82342355798844914df5b2d58cf17ebb8467c9
    # Since #34366, init_statics builds tables at startup and leaks them with Box::leak(). Using associated constants `BindingTables<D>` allows us to remove the ThreadUnsafeOnceLock lazy initialization and `unsafe` usage.
    # 
    # The core idea here, is that while statics can't be generic, associated constants can be. They will be relocated upon startup and are placed in the dyn.rel.ro section. With RELR enabled (#49099) this shrinks the binary size in production builds on ohos by ~1MB. Linux filesize might increase slightly or stay the same until https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/49129 is implemented.
    # 
    # We can't apply this to all the statics though, since `const`s are not guaranteed to be at a specific place in memory (you can have multiple), and we do rely on `ptr::eq` in some cases to compare equivalence, so we can't switch to `const`s in those cases.
    # 
    # Testing: Covered by existing tests. 
    # Part of #49095
    # 
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47459	(@Psychpsyo, #47459)	script: Implement `outdent` editing command (#47459)
    dom wip; rich text editing (contenteditable and execCommand, behind dom_exec_command_enabled): the caret is now shown, the outdent command, and many line break fixes
    ^ commit 1890345105cb3c1da550346a4a528538d996f819
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # This implements the `outdent` command.
    # 
    # Testing: More WPT passes
    # 
    # There is some interesting behavior that's still making some of the test fail though. Consider this setup:
    # ```html
    # <!DOCTYPE html>
    # <div contenteditable="true">
    #   <ol>
    #     <li>foo
    #     <ol>
    #       <li id="target">bar
    #     </ol>
    #     <li>baz
    #   </ol>
    # </div>
    # <script>
    #   document.getSelection().collapse(target.firstChild, 1);
    #   document.getSelection().extend(target.firstChild, 2);
    #   document.execCommand("outdent");
    # </script>
    # ```
    # Here we're trying to outdent an LI inside of a list.
    # WPT asserts, that the entire nested OL should just be gotten rid of. This makes sense and matches Chrome. (Firefox also removes the OL but then additionally merges the LI with the preceding one)
    # 
    # But from what I can tell, the spec as written accomplishes none of that:
    # The outdent command first block-extends the range (step 3), which means that it now wraps the inner LI.
    # Then, (step 5) it makes a list of all 'leaf' nodes in the range. (A leaf node being one with no further editable children, or, alternatively, an OL or UL or **correctly parented LI**)
    # In our example, *node list* contains only the LI now.
    # And now, (step 6.1) the spec specifically does not outdent children of OL/UL elements... which includes our LI.
    # So `outdent` is never called.
    # 
    # Not sure what to make of this yet, but I'll investigate it a bit more to see how the WPT tests ended up that way. (and maybe go and compare homework with Ladybird to see if they ended up doing anything different here.)

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49366	(@Loirooriol, #49366)	layout: Fixes for the percentage height quirk (#49366)
    layout; fixed several layout bugs in quirks mode, around percentage heights and margin collapsing
    ^ commit 6d3581dc86f1120c187b63b416a010fae43f811a
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # This implements 4 changes:
    # - Restricts the quirk to elements that have a percentage in `height`. E.g. `100%` won't be quirky in `height: auto; min-height: 100%`, but it will in `height: 0%; min-height: 100%`. This matches the spec and Gecko, though Blink and WebKit only enforce it on replaced elements.
    #   https://github.com/whatwg/quirks/issues/95
    # - More concretely, it requires a `<percentage>`, as opposed to a `<length-percentage>` containing a percentage. E.g. `calc(100% + 1px)` won't be quirky, but `100%`, `calc(100%)` and `calc(100% + 0px)` will. This matches the spec and Gecko, but not Blink and WebKit.
    #   https://github.com/whatwg/quirks/issues/48
    # - Restricts the quirk to percentage resolution. E.g. `height: stretch` won't be quirky. This matches Blink, the spec is unclear.
    #   https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12421
    # - Restricts the quirk to sizing purposes. E.g. `top: 100%` won't be quirky. This matches the spec, Blink and WebKit, but not Gecko.
    #   https://github.com/whatwg/quirks/issues/94
    # 
    # Testing: 2 existing tests improve, also adding new tests
    # Fixes: #49025
+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49581	(@gabrielecabrini, #49581)	script: Don't panic when dirtying a parentless text node during a move (#49581)
    crash; fixed crashes in replaceChild() and moveBefore() with shadow DOM
    ^ commit 1f95cb7ec864c9d4149236e53f1a452ef83cc94a
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # `moveBefore` detaches the node from its old parent but keeps it connected. For a slotted text node, slot assignment then calls `Node::dirty` while it has no parent, and the `unwrap()` there panicked.
    # 
    # Skip dirtying the parent when there is none; both the old and new parents are already dirtied during the move.
    # 
    # Testing: Added a WPT crashtest.
    # Fixes: #49532

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49582	(@gabrielecabrini, #49582)	webgpu: Register invalid ids when texture view or bind group layout creation fails (#49582)
    crash; fixed crashes in WebGPU createView() and createBindGroupLayout() with invalid arguments
    ^ commit f419768b95b0e43a09e32cb124da2ea25076494f
    # When `createView` or `createBindGroupLayout` got an invalid descriptor, the resource was never created in wgpu, so later uses of its id panicked in the WebGPU thread and then crashed the script thread.
    # 
    # Texture view descriptors are now validated by wgpu itself, and an invalid bind group layout is registered when script has already reported an error. Later uses now produce validation errors instead of panics.
    # 
    # Testing: Manually tested with the reproducer from the issue; WebGPU CTS createView and createBindGroupLayout tests are unchanged.
    # Fixes: #49537

    # During a single iteration of `TreeIterator`, no garbage collection takes
    # places, so it is safe to use `UnrootedDom`. Although we do not have the
    # guarantee of the Rust type system the performance benefits of this are
    # hard to ignore.
    # 
    # This eliminates > 30% of the rooting that happens in Speedometer 3,
    # for about a 1% performance gain. While the change to Speedometer is
    # within the range of noise, this shows a much more distinct improvement
    # on microbenchmarks. I think the elimination of over 30% of the rooting
    # is a good improvement in-and-of itself.
    # 
    # Testing: This is a performance improvement so we really only test this
    # via automated benchmarking.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49436	(@SharanRP, #49436)	webgl: zero initialize texStorage2D and texStorage3D  (#49436)

    ^ commit c2c5117bf158a5ff37076e43e20e9b8996c0748d
    # This change zero-initializes textures created with `texStorage2D` and `texStorage3D` to prevent GPU memory leaks. Instead of clearing through temporary `framebuffers` (which broke viewports, unbound user framebuffers, and failed on integer/float formats), it writes zero buffers directly using `texSubImage2D` and `texSubImage3D`. It temporarily unbinds pixel unpack buffers, resets unpack alignment, correctly handles all `WebGL2` sized formats, and ensures 2D texture array layer counts stay constant across mip levels.
    #

    # `compute_the_intersection` panicked when it unwrapped the frame element's `padding_box()`, which can be `None` for cases such as a 3D-transformed iframe.     ^ commit 13fbe153e23567be005a8ad079df58fb8677d044

    ^ /!\ contains servoshell changes! does it affect the user experience?
    # Not all text sent from an IME needs to go through a composition. The IME can handle that internally, sending only the final text, or e.g. the user selecting a word the IME guessed as the likely next word. In these cases it doesn't make sense to require the embedder to simulate a composition, when we can just insert the text directly. This matches behavior of other engines, see https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=1520983.

    ^ /!\ may contain changes to feature flags
    # Paint glyphs with `-webkit-text-fill-color` (resolved against `color`) instead of using `color` directly
    # (see: https://compat.spec.whatwg.org/#the-webkit-text-fill-color)
    # 
    # The feature is fully implemented (I think), but I have gated it behind a new `layout_css_webkit_text_fill_color_enabled` pref, because it is commonly used as a "poor mans `@supports()`" to set the text color to transparent in combination with `background-clip: text` in order to make the text invisible so that the glyph-clipped background can show through.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48031	(@Loirooriol, #48031)	Update Stylo to "Always borrow applicable declarations" (#48031)
    perf; faster style updates, and DOM elements use less memory
    ^ commit b2eb5c6240eec5a078bde23a55abfca147e176bc
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Mainly, the problem is that the change upstream changed `ApplicableDeclarationBlock` to contain a `StyleSourceBorrow` instead of a `StyleSource`. And this impacts `synthesize_presentational_hints_for_legacy_attributes()`, since it uses `ApplicableDeclarationBlock`.
    # 
    # This requires refactoring the computation of presentational hints to something closer to Gecko: instead of producing them on the fly during style resolution, we now compute them beforehand, and cache them.
    # 
    # Caching them directly on `Element` would have increased its size. So again I used an approach similar to Gecko: the `AttributeStorage` will now use a `HeaderSlice` instead of a `Vec`. This way we can store the presentational hints in the header. In fact, this approach *reduces* the size of `Element`.
    # 
    # Testing: No behavior change, but adding 2 new tests that would have crashed or failed with previous iterations of this patch. Unit tests check ensure that `Element` is now smaller.
    # 
    # Stylo PR: https://github.com/servo/stylo/pull/461
    #

    # Converting a node's type for layout used to hit `unreachable!` for any node that wasn't an element or a text node. The `HTMLOptGroupElement` branch of the `innerText` rendered text collection steps inspected the type of an ancestor, and when the optgroup had been appended directly to the `Document`, that ancestor was the `Document` itself, which caused a panic.
    #

    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49689	(@msub2, #49689)	net+script: Correct MIME type serialization and Document.contentType (#49689)
    dom; fixes to MIME type handling and document.contentType
    ^ commit caee2c181e79a8a88b9e6feada8d30b0a8ae05e5
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Previously, `extract_mime_type_as_mime` was manually serializing the DataUrlMime into a string, but the `data-url` crate [already has a serialization method](https://github.com/servo/rust-url/blob/main/data-url/src/mime.rs#L164) that appears to be spec-compliant, so this switches to using that instead.
    # 
    # Additionally, this change also exposed an issue with `Document.contentType`, which to this point had been taking the serialized Mime object in its entirety, parameters included. While not explicitly stated, every indication in the [HTML spec](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-document-content-type) seems to suggest that `contentType` should correspond to just the type/subtype of the Mime object we're using to represent `contentType`, which it now does.
    # 
    # Testing: 2 new passing WPT tests (`dom/nodes/Document-contentType/contentType/contenttype_datauri_02.html`, `encoding/single-byte-decoder-iframe.window.js`), 7 new subtest passes for `mimesniff/mime-types/charset-parameter.window.js`, 12 for `fetch/content-type/response.window.js`
    # 
    # Try run on my fork: https://github.com/msub2/servo/actions/runs/42404337904
    #

    ^ commit efb287b608337396670d982c9a491d971c6f622d
    # Bumps [aws-lc-rs](https://github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs) from 1.18.2 to 1.19.0.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/releases">aws-lc-rs's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>aws-lc-rs v1.19.0</h2>
    # <h2>What's Changed</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Add AES OFB, CFB8, and CFB1 modes by <a href="https://github.com/lokeswarivejju"><code>@​lokeswarivejju</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1259">aws/aws-lc-rs#1259</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Adds <code>OperatingMode::OFB</code>, <code>OperatingMode::CFB8</code>, and <code>OperatingMode::CFB1</code> for AES-128, AES-192, and AES-256 through both the one-shot and streaming cipher APIs.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li>Add an on-demand AWS-LC self-test API by <a href="https://github.com/Elvrarin"><code>@​Elvrarin</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1262">aws/aws-lc-rs#1262</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Adds <code>aws_lc_rs::try_fips_self_test()</code>, allowing applications to re-run all AWS-LC cryptographic algorithm self-tests without calling the sys crate through unsafe FFI. This does not re-run the module integrity test.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li>Add  Keccak-256 digest support behind <em>&quot;unstable&quot;</em> feature flag by <a href="https://github.com/fabrice102"><code>@​fabrice102</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1266">aws/aws-lc-rs#1266</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Adds <code>aws_lc_rs::unstable::digest::KECCAK256</code> and <code>KECCAK256_OUTPUT_LEN</code> behind the <code>unstable</code> feature for use with the existing <code>digest::digest</code> and <code>digest::Context</code> APIs.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li>Add configurable RSA-PSS salt lengths by <a href="https://github.com/PaulDance"><code>@​PaulDance</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1267">aws/aws-lc-rs#1267</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Adds <code>RsaPssSaltLength::{Digest, Max, Custom}</code> and <code>with_pss_salt_length</code> builders on <code>RsaSignatureEncoding</code> and <code>RsaParameters</code>, covering signing and verification for both message and precomputed-digest APIs.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li>Implement <code>Clone</code> for signing key pairs by <a href="https://github.com/JakeHuneau"><code>@​JakeHuneau</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1277">aws/aws-lc-rs#1277</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>rsa::KeyPair</code>, <code>EcdsaKeyPair</code>, <code>Ed25519KeyPair</code>, and <code>PqdsaKeyPair</code> can now be cloned directly rather than wrapped in an <code>Arc</code>.</li>
    # <li>Clones share the immutable, reference-counted <code>EVP_PKEY</code>; key material is not duplicated, and a clone remains usable after the original is dropped.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Upstream AWS-LC</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>aws-lc-sys</code> v0.47.0 aligns with <a href="https://github.com/aws/aws-lc/releases/tag/v5.12.0">AWS-LC v5.12.0</a> (previously v5.11.0).</li>
    # <li><code>aws-lc-fips-sys</code> v0.14.4 updates its bundled AWS-LC FIPS 4.2.0 snapshot with recent FIPS 4.x backports.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Build Improvements</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Lower the minimum system-installed AWS-LC version to 4.2.0 by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1268">aws/aws-lc-rs#1268</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>The default <code>aws-lc-sys</code> system-library path now accepts AWS-LC 4.2.0 or newer, previously 5.7.0, allowing use of a FIPS 4.x system library.</li>
    # <li>Enabling the <code>unstable</code> feature requires AWS-LC 5.7.0 or newer because unstable APIs currently include Keccak-256. Bundled-source builds are unaffected.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li>Share <code>cc</code> compiler detection across builds by <a href="https://github.com/johncarmack1984"><code>@​johncarmack1984</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1273">aws/aws-lc-rs#1273</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Reuses compiler detection results across the main library build and compiler feature probes, reducing repeated compiler invocations during cold builds.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Issues Being Closed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Implement <code>Clone</code> for <code>rsa::KeyPair</code> -- <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/issues/1260">aws/aws-lc-rs#1260</a></li>
    # <li>Expose AWS-LC FIPS on-demand self-test -- <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/issues/1261">aws/aws-lc-rs#1261</a></li>
    # <li>Add a way to specify the RSA-PSS salt length -- <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/issues/1263">aws/aws-lc-rs#1263</a></li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>Other Merged PRs</h2>

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49699	(@rogerkorantenng, #49699)	script: Keep WebDriver JSON clone cycle detection valid across GC (#49699)
    ^ commit 610e881f5cb5c470aee49d4cfe2131505d18d3aa
    # The WebDriver JSON clone stored visited objects as raw `JSVal` bits in a `HashSet` to detect cycles. The walk runs JavaScript (`toJSON` and getters), so a GC can move objects and make those bits stale. This change stores them in a `RootedVec<Box<Heap<JSVal>>>` instead, the same as the console fix in #49697. It also matches the spec, where `seen` is a list that is appended to and has its last element removed.
    # 
    # Testing: No new test, because a test can't force a GC at this point in the walk, and on main a forced GC still catches the cycle one level later. The WebDriver `execute_script` and `execute_async_script` tests (including `cyclic.py`) give the same results as main. `./mach test-devtools`, `./mach test-unit` and `./mach check --use-crown` pass.
    # 
    # Note: This is the WebDriver part of #47778. It is separate from #49697 because @jdm asked for it to be fixed in its own PR in #49667.
    # 
    # Fixes: #47778
-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49697	(@rogerkorantenng, #49697)	script: Keep console cycle detection valid across GC (#49697)
    ^ commit d6c17f7524e428e9605400ba3fc972105291793d
    # The console stored visited objects as raw `JSVal` bits to detect cycles. A GC during the walk can move objects and make those bits stale. This change stores them in a `RootedVec<Box<Heap<JSVal>>>` instead, which the GC keeps up to date. The WebDriver code has the same bug and will be fixed in a separate PR.
    # 
    # Testing: No new test, because a test can't force a GC at this point in the walk. With a temporary `JS_GC` call added during the walk, 13 console devtools tests fail on main and all pass with this change. `./mach test-devtools`, `./mach test-unit` and `./mach check --use-crown` pass.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49745	(@Loirooriol, #49745)	layout: Prevent widgets from obeying layout-internal display types (#49745)
    layout; form controls and replaced elements with display: table-cell and similar values now behave like in other browsers
    ^ commit 5156122c1fe8e924c787aff5bc464239f71bbdd3
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # We were already preventing most widgets like `<input>` from using `display` values like `flex` or `inline-grid`. However, we were allowing layout-internal values like `table-cell`, even though all browsers agree these should be treated as `inline`/`inline-block`, like it happens for fully replaced elements.
    # 
    # `<button>` is more permissive, so we were already allowing values like `flex` or `inline-grid`, as expected. But again we were also allowing layout-internal values like `table-cell`, which shouldn't happen.
    # 
    # Therefore, this patch refactors `DisplayGeneratingBox::used_value_for_contents()` to use the same logic for replaced elements and widgets. More precisely, the changes are:
    # 
    # - Widgets with a layout-internal display will now behave as `inline-block`, instead of as the computed value.
    # - Replaced elements with a layout-internal display will now behave as `inline-block`, instead of as `inline`. Doesn't really matter in practice, since `inline` was already forced to be atomic like `inline-block`.
    # - Replaced elements with a non-internal display will now behave as `inline-block`, instead of as the computed value. Doesn't really matter in practice at the moment, but in particular they will no longer be list items, which might become observable once we implement counters.
    # 
    # Testing: Adding a new test. Another existing test now passes. Some new failures in `/html/rendering/widgets/button-layout/display-other.html`, but these fail on all browsers, the test seems wrong.
    # Fixes: #49744
    #

    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    ^ /!\ contains servoshell changes! does it affect the user experience?
    # This changes the ResourceReader to return ResourceBytes which is a thin wrapper around `bytes::Bytes`. The advantage is that bytes can take mmap files without copying the underlying bytes and just keeps the mmap alive.
    # This reduces the allocation by roughly 2mb.

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49782	(@mrobinson, #49782)	wpt: Generate `MANIFEST.json` files dynamically (#49782)
    ^ commit 78fc03d210413da734f97ff195903e1892834890
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Instead of checking in our `MANIFEST.json` files, generate them
    # incrementally when running WPT tests. For subsequent runs, a hash is
    # kept to determine when to run incremental updates. Only when the hash
    # differs do we update manifests. This means that for most instances of
    # running WPT tests, people should not notice a large difference in test
    # runtimes. This is the approach used by Chromium.
    # 
    # In addition, this allows removing all tidy checks relating to
    # `MANIFEST.json` files and avoids developer errors related to submitting
    # changes without manifest updates.
    # 
    # For a fresh checkout this introduces a ~20 second delay in running
    # tests. Since this adds a delay to all WPT jobs, a followup
    # change will either:
    #  - Try to pass the generated `MANIFEST.json` files to each WPT job.
    #  - Keep a cached version of `MANIFEST.json` checked-in, which is only
    #    updated during the weekly WPT sync. Further incremental updates
    #    would be faster.
    # 
    # Testing: Running the WPT tests properly is really the test for this.
    # Closes: #23214.
    # Closes: #23213.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49821	(@Daniel-1600, #49821)	script: Implement console.dirxml (#49821)

    ^ commit 8036fd430c7dc749f28134ce3a6d77cab561da3b

    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49759	(@jdm, #49759)	script: Move all WASM streaming logic out of Response. (#49759)
    dom; WebAssembly.compileStreaming() and instantiateStreaming() now work with any Response, not just network responses
    ^ commit 9679322d6fc1e02030c7f2a7481e74f416dce033
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Our existing implementation was tied to the fetch network listener, so any response that came directly from the network and was passed to compileStreaming/instantiateStreaming worked fine. We did not have any mechanism to trigger reads on arbitrary stream inputs, which explains the many test failures and timeouts involving non-fetch responses. These changes remove all WASM stream consumer integration from Response and make it the responsibility of the WASM stream consumer runtime hook.
    # 
    # The implementation in this PR is far from optimal—not only does read_all_bytes use a lot of promise machinery internally that are unnecessary for the native stream integration, but it also only provides a single callback once the entire stream contents are available in a single buffer, defeating the purpose of streaming. This should be addressed separately in https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/49758 but should not block merging this working implementation.
    # 
    # Depends on #49757.
    # 
    # Testing: Look at all those new passing tests.
    # Fixes: #49733
    # Fixes: #41790
    # Fixes: #40013
    # Fixes: #39966
    # Closes: #41792

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49839	(@mwja, #49839)	layout: Round scroll area requests correctly so fractional offset does not result in incorrect result (#49839)
    layout; fixes to scrollHeight rounding and <textarea> height
    ^ commit 157e70b4188d9d1a0a53afe3c334f970ec64976d
    # Currently, `scrollHeight` on the JS side is retrieved (through a chain of calls) from a `Rect` that is rounded incorrectly. This causes inconsistencies with other engines - and unexpected values (only off by one px, though). This means `scrollHeight` and `clientHeight` can differ just due to the position of the measured node.
    # 
    # This changes that, to round origin and size independently so a fractional offset of the node does not result in inconsistent rounding of the scroll area.
    # 
    # Old behaviour:
    # <img width="2545" height="606" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/1e395735-9d73-44c2-95e9-297d9a2375ad" />
    # 
    # New behaviour:
    # <img width="1477" height="236" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/14801a06-f41f-491b-9225-d5a72f3c3d0e" />
    # 
    # 
    # Testing: WPT run locally, passing where expected. New `scrollWidthHeight-subpixel-position.tentative.html` to test this case specifically, and its linked to https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/9866 which covers a similar discussion. I can confirm the test fails on `main` (20/36 fail) and passes on my branch.

    ^ commit 5d4bf14d2ce66b828e1602542e670db824945db4
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    ^ /!\ contains servoshell changes! does it affect the user experience?
    # The key problem this is aiming to solve is that we currently have no way to mark the webview's focused node as the focused node for the servoshell application, from the perspective of AccessKit.
    # 
    # This change adds a `WebViewWidget` widget which can request focus from egui, which allows focus to be moved into the webview from servoshell's egui-based UI using the keyboard, and allows the AccessKit graft node for the webview to be marked as the focused node for egui-winit's accesskit tree.
    # 
    # Note that the graft node *must* be the AccessKit node for the widget with egui focus in order for AccessKit focus to work correctly.
    # 
    # Testing: Manual testing: with a screen reader running, move focus into and out of web contents using keyboard (tab key) and using mouse, and switch between applications while focus is in the webview. The focused node should be correctly reflected in the screen reader's "locus of focus", and the application should be stable when switching between applications. (Known issue: focusing the location bar with a screen reader running is *very* slow to update screen reader focus; this seems to be a pre-existing issue in egui.)
    # Fixes: #49565, #49567

    # This fixes an assertion being hit when viewbox is negative such as in this example
    # `<svg viewBox="0 0 -1 1" style="object-fit: cover; width: 100px; height: 50px"></svg>`.
    # 
    # The spec says https://www.w3.org/TR/SVG11/coords.html#ViewBoxAttribute that zero skips the viewbox (which I assume is done by returning None) while negative values for width or height should have error.
    # 
    # We solve this by storing the viewbox ratio inside the svg element, checking it on attribute parsing. The attribute ratio can be in 3 separate states, Normal, empty and invalid.
    # 
    # Testing: This was tested on the above fragment which was also added as a crashtest.

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49877	(@Loirooriol, #49877)	layout: Make it hard to accidentally use a quirky size (#49877)
    ^ commit e0c8a4452504661fe61842931e36830211899a1d
    # It was easy for some code to read `containing_block.size.block` without remembering to check `has_quirky_block_size`. This could result in wrong behavior when in quirks mode.
    # 
    # Therefore, this renames `IndefiniteContainingBlockSize`'s `block`, `ContainingBlockSize`'s `block`, and `ConstraintSpace`'s `block_size` into `maybe_quirky_block_size`. For consistency, `inline` is renamed to `inline_size`.
    # 
    # For ease of use, `IndefiniteContainingBlock`, `ContainingBlock` and `ConstraintSpace` gain new methods to access the desired value. `block_size()` avoids the quirk, `maybe_quirky_block_size()` doesn't.
    # 
    # Also, since the quirk only affects block and inline layout, this removes the `has_quirky_block_size` parameter for `new()`. Instead, `new()` is non-quirky, while `new_quirky()` is quirky. This means that flex and grid layouts can forget that the quirk exists for the most part.
    # 
    # While `containing_block.rs` grows larger due to the new methods, the other files actually have a net reduction of 50 lines due to simpler logic.
    # 
    # There should be no behavior change in practice, but this fixes some logic for table layout that was generating a `ContainingBlockSize` with `HasQuirkyBlockSize(false)`, and a block size coming from another containing block which might actually be quirky. This was only used for logical↔physical mappings that ignored this block size, so no real bug.
    # 
    # Testing: Not needed, no behavior change
    #

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49895	(@jdm, #49895)	wpt: Make de-linking regex less restrictive. (#49895)
    ^ commit 883084c40e88aa4014c2956adb2b45d7d059d2d4
    # As reported in https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/issues/64986, we had an exported PR which unintentionally closed an issue in the WPT repo because the exported body included `Fixes: [#49520](https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/49520)`. This construction did not match our filtering regex that tries to prevent it, so the regex needs to accept non-word characters directly adjacent to the # character. This makes it much harder to tell if it's a reference like `servo/servo#123`, which would not cause a problem, so these changes make those references unlinkable as well.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49801	(@rogerkorantenng, #49801)	devtools: Use real parameter names in console function previews (#49801)
    devtools; the Console now shows DOM objects like document and navigator, and functions with their real parameter names
    ^ commit a46f112fd11aed77d788570e0c41b9211ae675c5
    ^ /!\ contains WebIDL changes! did we ship a new API?
    # When you log a function, the devtools preview showed made-up parameter names like `<arg0>` and `<arg1>`. The console code can't use the Debugger API, so this follows the approach suggested in #49667 and asks debugger.js for the names instead.
    # 
    # debugger.js now registers a callback on `DebuggerGlobalScope` when it starts. The callback takes a function and returns `Debugger.Object.parameterNames`. `DebuggerGlobalScope` keeps it as a `TracedCallback`, and `console.rs` calls it through the new `GlobalScope::debugger_global()`, which finds the debugger global for a window or a worker. If there is no debugger global, the preview still falls back to `<argN>`.
    # 
    # Note: This builds on #49697. Calling into debugger.js can trigger a GC, and before #49697 that GC broke the console's cycle detection.
    # 
    # Testing: `test_console_log_function_arguments` is no longer marked as expected to fail, and I added a test for `function (a, b = 1, ...rest)`. When I removed the callback registration on purpose, both tests failed with `<arg0>`, `<arg1>`, so they do test the new code. I also logged functions from a worker with devtools on, with no panics. `./mach test-tidy`, `./mach test-unit`, `./mach test-devtools` and `./mach check --use-crown` pass, with no new warnings.
    # 
    # Fixes: #49667

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49902	(@mxmgorin, #49902)	webgl: Accept TEXTURE_2D_ARRAY layers in framebufferTextureLayer (#49902)
    dom; many WebGL and WebGL 2 fixes, including zero-initialised texture storage and non-power-of-two mipmaps
    ^ commit 23b0581548c1bddb85c9c040ac629d3153f7a3b6
    # This makes `framebufferTextureLayer` accept `TEXTURE_2D_ARRAY` layers, including the last valid mipmap level and layer, and update the framebuffer status after attaching a layer, as required by the [OpenGL ES 3.0 spec](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#page=221) (4.4.2.4). Previously, it checked for `TEXTURE_2D` instead, causing array layers to be rejected with `INVALID_OPERATION`. It also failed to update the framebuffer status after attaching a layer.
    # 
    # `checkFramebufferStatus` now returns `FRAMEBUFFER_UNSUPPORTED` for [mismatched depth and stencil images](https://registry.khronos.org/webgl/specs/latest/2.0/#FBO_ATTACHMENTS) and [shared color attachment images](https://registry.khronos.org/webgl/specs/latest/2.0/#FRAMEBUFFER_COLOR_ATTACHMENTS).
    # 
    # `framebufferTextureLayer` now also generates `INVALID_OPERATION` on an [opaque WebXR framebuffer](https://immersive-web.github.io/webxr/#opaque-framebuffer), like `framebufferTexture2D`.
    # 
    # Testing: More conformance tests now pass.
    # Part of #37738.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49800	(@yug105, #49800)	script: Implement lazy loading for srcdoc iframes (#49800)
    dom; lazy loading now works for <iframe srcdoc>
    ^ commit affba180d67dd7fc34d67947e0775869fccefd3c
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Below-viewport iframes with `loading="lazy"` and a `srcdoc` attribute never loaded. Servo already stored the lazy load resumption steps, but there was a TODO where the spec says to start intersection-observing the element. Because of that, nothing ever triggered those steps.
    # 
    # This PR adds an internal intersection observer for lazy loading. IntersectionObserver callbacks can now be either script callbacks or internal callbacks (`IntersectionObserverCallbackKind`). A Rust-only constructor creates the lazy load observer with a 600px scroll margin, the same 600px Firefox uses. The spec leaves this margin to individual browsers. Each Document gets its own lazy load observer, along with the spec's "start intersection-observing" and "stop intersection-observing" helpers. When an observed iframe intersects, it is removed from observation and its resumption steps run, navigating it to `srcdoc`. When an iframe starts a navigation while lazy, it is also removed from observation and its resumption steps are cleared, as required by HTML navigate, step 11. This prevents old `srcdoc` steps from running if the iframe is switched to `src` before it loads.
    # 
    # The internal observer works even when `dom_intersection_observer_enabled` is disabled. That preference only controls the JavaScript IntersectionObserver interface.
    # 
    # `iframe-loading-lazy.html` went from TIMEOUT to OK, and the "Below-viewport srcdoc iframes load lazily" subtest now passes. The other three subtests (`src`, `data:` and `blob:` URLs) still fail, as before. `iframe-loading-lazy-to-eager-srcdoc.html` runs as expected.
    # 
    # In `the-iframe-element/` (167 tests) and `intersection-observer/` (157 tests), everything ran as expected except `sandbox_002.htm` and `v2/animated-opacity.html`. `sandbox_002.htm` crashes locally with the dummy media backend; it uses video and has no connection to lazy loading or `srcdoc`. Its `.ini` expects OK, so it presumably passes on CI with real media available. `v2/animated-opacity.html` is flaky and passed 2 out of 3 times when run on its own.
    # 
    # I also manually checked the stale-steps case: switching to `src` before scrolling now results in one load instead of two.
    # 
    # Working on this also exposed a panic in `compute_the_intersection`, which was fixed separately in #49407.
    # 
    # Fixes: #47287

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49735	(@veyndan, #49735)	android: Let app decide whether to optimise library (#49735)
    servoshell; Android: modernised Gradle build and many cleanups
    ^ commit 76c5723bfe48a2964522f0fb86d6d7ae7fa7b167
    # Based off of the [documentation](https://developer.android.com/topic/performance/app-optimization/library-optimization#optimize-aar), attempting to optimize at the library level isn't necessary nor recommended:
    # 
    # > Note: A common misconception in Android development is that optimizing your AAR (Android Archive) or JAR (Java Archive) makes it acceptable to prevent optimization by keeping your library package during the app-level build. However, whole-program optimization (at the app level) is significantly more effective than optimization done at the library level. It's only during an app build, when a library is included as part of an app, that R8 can know how all the methods of the library are used, and which parameters are passed.
    # 
    # Testing: There are no automated tests for Android.
    # 
+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49812	(@mrobinson, #49812)	servoshell: Initialize egui with the device pixel ratio override (#49812)
    servoshell; the browser UI now respects --device-pixel-ratio from startup
    ^ commit d2dd8f3dd57a4a03edbb9cba420ecf69ab729960
    ^ /!\ contains servoshell changes! does it affect the user experience?
    # This fixes an immediate panic (seemingly unrelated to #49768) on macOS
    # when passing a `--device-pixel-ratio` override. The issue is that when
    # you set a pending zoom change in egui. When this happens egui tries to
    # use the previous frame's pending size in order to avoid jitter. Since
    # there is no pending frame when this is done before the first frame, the
    # frame size placeholder is used. This placeholder is 10000 pixels square
    # which trigger the creation of a `RenderingContext` that is too big.
    # 
    # Testing: We don't really have testing at this layer of servoshell.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49905	(@Daniel-1600, #49905)	script: Implement FontFaceSet.status (#49905)
    dom; FontFaceSet.status, FontFaceSetLoadEvent and the loading, loadingdone and loadingerror events, and FontFace and FontFaceSet in workers
    ^ commit d7d6eaa3feb3510861831e24cf6483b3f989feeb
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    ^ /!\ contains WebIDL changes! did we ship a new API?
    # Added the `status` attribute to FontFaceSet. It is set to "loading" when the set switches to loading, and to "loaded" right before the ready promise is fulfilled, following the "switch the FontFaceSet to loaded" algorithm.
    # 
    # The specification does not define an initial value. Since the ready promise starts out pending, start out as "loading" so that `status` is "loaded" exactly when the ready promise is fulfilled.
    # 
    # Testing: This change makes the following WPT tests pass:
    # - css/css-font-loading/fontface-fonts-loading.html
    # - css/css-font-loading/fontfaceset-ready-unused-face-failed-local-source.html
    # - css/css-font-loading/idlharness.https.html (the two `status` subtests)
    # Part of #29376.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49914	(@veyndan, #49914)	android: Remove needless signing of library (#49914)
    servoshell; Android: modernised Gradle build and many cleanups
    ^ commit 62af16cb2c4ed3c0b4d251604972fb90708bdba5
    # I was a bit baffled as to why the comment suggests we need to sign the library. Searching around, I couldn't find an answer, so I asked in the [Android Study Group Slack channel](https://androidstudygroup.slack.com/archives/C03KKLGC2/p1794247417588439) (unfortunately invite only). The summary of the conversation is that signing libraries are only "used by the test APK".
    # 
    # Since the only time the test APK would need to be signed is maybe if we uploaded the APK to somewhere like Firebase Test Lab to test the library on an emulator/real device. We don't do this, nor do I think we'd ever want to do this for the library by itself (we'd at most want to do it at the app level).
    # 
    # All that's to say is that signing the library is redundant currently and can be removed.
    # 
    # Testing: A successful CI compilation is enough to test this.
    #

    # Bumps [arc-swap](https://github.com/vorner/arc-swap) from 1.9.3 to 1.9.4.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/vorner/arc-swap/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">arc-swap's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h1>1.9.4</h1>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fix use-after-free when strong and weak pointers to the same allocation are
    # stored in different instances (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/vorner/arc-swap/issues/214">#214</a>). Adds <code>RefCnt::TAG</code> (defaults to 0, so
    # existing implementations still compile); storing a pointer with the tag bits
    # set panics (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/vorner/arc-swap/issues/214">#214</a>).</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>

    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/PolyMeilex/rfd/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">rfd's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>0.18.0</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fix some GTK3 memory leaks</li>
    # <li>Add GTK4 backend behind the <code>gtk4</code> feature</li>
    # <li>MSRV is now 1.92</li>
    # <li>Fix macOS file dialogs ignoring <code>set_directory</code> when <code>set_file_name</code> is also used.</li>
    # <li>Add <code>FileDialog::set_show_hidden_files</code> and <code>AsyncFileDialog::set_show_hidden_files</code> to control hidden file visibility. Supported on macOS, Windows, and Linux (GTK3).</li>
    # <li>Honor <code>FileDialog::set_directory</code> and <code>AsyncFileDialog::set_directory</code> when using the zenity fallback backend on Linux.</li>
    # <li>Fix <code>liblary</code> typo in docs</li>
    # <li>Change wasm title to sanitize text contents via <code>title_el.set_text_content()</code> rather than <code>title_el.set_inner_html()</code>.</li>
    # <li>Set wasm dialog styles via <code>set_text_content()</code> rather than <code>set_inner_html()</code>, so rfd no longer uses <code>innerHTML</code>.</li>
    # <li>Fix GTK3 message dialogs using the description as a printf format string, which garbled text containing <code>%</code> and could crash. The description is now shown as given, so <code>%%</code> is no longer shown as <code>%</code>.</li>
    # <li>Support files without an extension in GTK3 and zenity filters, as the XDG portal backend does since <a href="https://redirect.github.com/PolyMeilex/rfd/issues/215">#215</a>. A match-all filter (<code>&quot;*&quot;</code>) or an empty one (<code>&quot;&quot;</code>) now uses the glob <code>&quot;*&quot;</code>, not <code>&quot;*.*&quot;</code> or <code>&quot;*.&quot;</code>, so a filter such as &quot;All files&quot; matches a name with no dot, like <code>README</code>.</li>
    # <li>Zenity now returns correct paths when a name contains <code>|</code>. Also file names that start or end with a space are kept intact.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49912	(@mxmgorin, #49912)	webgl: Reject disabled compressed and 3D depth formats in texStorage (#49912)
    dom; many WebGL and WebGL 2 fixes, including zero-initialised texture storage and non-power-of-two mipmaps
    ^ commit 43753d9b4a9c16e5b473c2df3703daf0239651ca
    # This makes `texStorage2D` and `texStorage3D` validate the internal format like the other texture functions:
    # 
    # * Compressed formats generate `INVALID_ENUM` until their extension is enabled. The [WebGL spec](https://registry.khronos.org/webgl/specs/latest/1.0/#5.14.14) (5.14.14) requires an error when an extension is used before it is enabled, and the [OpenGL ES 3.0 spec](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#nameddest=section.2.5) (2.5) makes that `INVALID_ENUM` for an enum the command does not accept. `compressedTexImage2D` already does this.
    # * Depth and depth-stencil formats generate `INVALID_OPERATION` with `TEXTURE_3D`, as required by the [OpenGL ES 3.0 spec](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#nameddest=subsection.3.8.3) (3.8.3, applied to `texStorage*` by [3.8.4](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#nameddest=subsection.3.8.4)). `texImage3D` already does this.
    # 
    # Previously, both were accepted, so S3TC textures could be allocated with `texStorage*` before `WEBGL_compressed_texture_s3tc` was enabled.
    # 
    # Follow-up to #48590.
    # Testing: Adds `webgl/texStorage-format-checks.html`; three of its five subtests fail without this change.

    # Red -> Blue -> Green

    # This reduces image decoding time by 13–28% for images without an alpha channel by skipping premultiplication. Since every pixel in such an image is opaque, premultiplication has no effect.
    # 
    # Results of the new `load_from_memory` benchmark on an Intel Core i7-10750H, Linux, default x86_64 target:
    # 
    # | Image | `bench` profile (`opt-level = 3`) | `production` profile (`opt-level = "s"`, fat LTO) |
    # |---|---|---|
    # | JPEG | 50.8 -> 36.7 ms (-28%) | 50.8 -> 36.0 ms (-29%) |
    # | PNG without alpha | 58.4 -> 43.2 ms (-26%) | 78.8 -> 62.1 ms (-21%) |
    # | Lossy WebP | 68.1 -> 54.8 ms (-20%) | 115.3 -> 99.7 ms (-14%) |
    # | PNG with transparency (control, not skipped) | 20.7 -> 21.1 ms (within noise) | 21.6 -> 22.1 ms (within noise) |
    # 
    # To reproduce: `./mach exec cargo bench -p servo-pixels --bench load_from_memory`, with `--profile production` for the second column.
    # 
    # Testing: No behavior change. A new unit test covers images with and without  alpha.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48464	(@Narfinger, #48464)	script_bindings: Preallocate root vector (#48464)

    ^ commit 2eacc75f6e41dde4fbc3f915a7bd3d7f292688ae
    # This PR preallocates the root vector to 256 elements, increasing per thread size by roughly 32kb.
    # This, however, allows us to not have to resize the vector for a typical website. While the vector resize runtime impact is very small, repeated allocations in the small range can be very time sensitive.
    # This is a bit more inefficient because we cannot use const for thread_local anymore.
    # 
    # We opted against a prefs parameter as the associated RwLock::read() call would negate probably any speed increase we get.
    # 
    # Testing: This just influences allocation behavior.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49891	(@mrobinson, @Loirooriol, #49891)	script/paint: Make scroll ranges sensitive to writing modes (#49891)
    layout wip; progress on vertical writing modes (writing-mode): whole pages can now use a single writing mode, and scrolling, ResizeObserver, tables, flex, grid and replaced elements are being moved to logical coordinates
    ^ commit 76654a8d893db010d3091930696af7b8778cd5d4
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # This change makes various fixes to ensure that scroll ranges are
    # sensitive to the writing mode of their scrolling boxes. Scroll ranges
    # should be clamped to the edges of the content box, even if they extent
    # into negative values beyond the edges of the scrolling box (for RTL writing
    # modes). In addition, start and end scroll targets are now sensitive to
    # the writing mode (flipping in the case of RTL). Finally, scroll
    # positions queries no longer call `abs()` on their values, reflecting the
    # fact that scroll positions can be negative.
    # 
    # Testing: Change causes various WPT tests to start passing and one to start
    # failing due to us not implementing the resolution from w3c/csswg-drafts#12289
    # yet.
    #

    # Updates `webpki-root-certs` from 1.0.11 to 1.0.12
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rustls/webpki-roots/releases">webpki-root-certs's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>1.0.12</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>
    # <p>Remove &quot;Buypass Class 2 Root CA&quot; and &quot;Buypass Class 3 Root CA&quot;. Reference: <a href="https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2085917">https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2085917</a></p>
    # </li>
    # <li>
    # <p>Remove &quot;DigiCert Trusted Root G4&quot;, &quot;QuoVadis Root CA 1 G3&quot; and &quot;QuoVadis Root CA 3 G3&quot; (as they are now trusted only for email). Reference: <a href="https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2088266">https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2088266</a></p>
    # </li>
    # <li>
    # <p>Remove &quot;Certigna Root CA&quot;. Reference: <a href="https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2085928">https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=2085928</a></p>
    # </li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>What's Changed</h2>
    # <ul>

    # <li>1.0.12: track roots removed upstream by <a href="https://github.com/ctz"><code>@​ctz</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rustls/webpki-roots/pull/139">rustls/webpki-roots#139</a></li>

    ^ commit 9af88e807f46cfa306795964ee8f9c0780f4f017
    # Bumps [rustls-pki-types](https://github.com/rustls/pki-types) from 1.15.1 to 1.16.0.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rustls/pki-types/releases">rustls-pki-types's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>1.16.0</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li><strong>Behavior change</strong> for edge case inputs: <code>DnsName</code> now rejects names whose final label is a hex number, like <code>0x7f000001</code> or <code>example.0x1f</code>.</li>
    # <li><strong>Behaviour change</strong>: <code>DnsName</code> and <code>ServerName</code> now strip a trailing dot to canonicalize names.</li>
    # <li><strong>New API</strong>: <code>DnsName::into_owned()</code> which can avoid copies.</li>
    # <li>Faster <code>DnsName</code> validation.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>What's Changed</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>chore: replace datatracker references, update I-D -&gt; RFC9881 by <a href="https://github.com/mcr"><code>@​mcr</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rustls/pki-types/pull/115">rustls/pki-types#115</a></li>
    # <li>Introduce <code>DnsName::into_owned()</code> by <a href="https://github.com/ctz"><code>@​ctz</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rustls/pki-types/pull/116">rustls/pki-types#116</a></li>
    # <li>Correct zero-check helper name and documentation by <a href="https://github.com/eliask"><code>@​eliask</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rustls/pki-types/pull/118">rustls/pki-types#118</a></li>
    # <li>pem: state correct 256 MB limit in the SectionTooLarge error message by <a href="https://github.com/herniqeu"><code>@​herniqeu</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rustls/pki-types/pull/119">rustls/pki-types#119</a></li>
    # <li>1.16.0: stricter and canonicalising <code>DnsName</code> by <a href="https://github.com/ctz"><code>@​ctz</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rustls/pki-types/pull/117">rustls/pki-types#117</a></li>
    # </ul>

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49953	(@mrobinson, #49953)	    script: Snap device pixels instead of rounding in `ResizeObserverEntry` (#49953)
    dom; ResizeObserver is now complete: more accurate device pixel sizes, less layout work, and other fixes
    ^ commit 4c79a5bc5bb837f50eada1ae66733ff1576f6180
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # `ResizeObserverEntry#devicePixelContentBoxSize` needs device widths. Before
    # Servo was simply rounding the width, but that doesn't take into account
    # where the start of the rectangle is on the screen. In this change, Servo
    # now snaps both the start and the end of the rectangle to the screen and
    # then caculates a true device pixel length.
    # 
    # Testing: This change gets a WPT test passing.
    #

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/50018	(@dependabot[bot], @dependabot[bot], #50018)	build: bump exr from 1.74.2 to 1.74.3 (#50018)
    ^ commit d5302b6aabc297207d8a69b1112328155217392c
    # Bumps [exr](https://github.com/johannesvollmer/exrs) from 1.74.2 to 1.74.3.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/johannesvollmer/exrs/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">exr's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>[1.74.3] - 2026-11-09</h2>
    # <h3>Changed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Use compiler autovectorization for DWA transforms, replacing handwritten SIMD kernels with batch-level AVX2 runtime dispatch via <code>multiversion</code>.</li>
    # <li>Speeds up the forward DCT of DWA compression by about 50x on ARM NEON.</li>
    # <li>Replaces the <code>pulp</code> and <code>num-complex</code> dependencies with <code>multiversion</code>, which is only used on x86 and x86-64.</li>
    # <li>Replaces the test-only cargo features <code>avx2-tests</code>, <code>sse2-tests</code> and <code>simd-benches</code> with a single <code>dct-benches</code> feature.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fixes a panic when decoding damaged B44 files, which aborted the whole process when decoding in parallel.</li>
    # <li>Fixes damaged DWA files making the Huffman decoder reserve huge amounts of memory up front, which aborted the whole process.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Commits</summary>
    # <ul>
    # <li>See full diff in <a href="https://github.com/johannesvollmer/exrs/commits">compare view</a></li>

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49750	(@mrobinson, #49750)	servo: Take WebView transforms and `<iframe>` nesting into account for embedder control rects (#49750)
    api; the positions of embedder-drawn controls (like <select> pickers) are now correct with pinch zoom, scrolling and iframes
    ^ commit 698737d93cab254b55fcd6484e48b6cc8464859e
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    ^ /!\ contains servoshell changes! does it affect the user experience?
    # This change makes it so that WebView transforms (pinch zoom, scrolling)
    # and `<iframe>` nesting are taken into account when computing embedder
    # control rectangles. Before these rectangles were totally incorrect. This
    # involves sending `<iframe>` data into `Paint` so that it can be used to
    # work out a cumulative transformation back into device pixels.
    # 
    # Testing: This change adds a suite of units tests.
    # Fixes: #42113.
    # 
+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/50034	(@mxmgorin, #50034)	webgl: Allow non-power-of-two mipmaps in WebGL 2 (#50034)
    dom; many WebGL and WebGL 2 fixes, including zero-initialised texture storage and non-power-of-two mipmaps
    ^ commit a0ab2840bd06d6600f709d20bb2caebef721c573
    # This makes `texImage2D` and related calls above level 0 ([3.7.1](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/2.0/es_full_spec_2.0.pdf#nameddest=subsection.3.7.1)) and `generateMipmap` ([3.7.11](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/2.0/es_full_spec_2.0.pdf#nameddest=subsection.3.7.11)) accept non-power-of-two sizes in WebGL 2. Both restrictions come from OpenGL ES 2.0 and were removed in [OpenGL ES 3.0](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#nameddest=section.E.1), so they apply to WebGL 1 only.
    # 
    # Testing: More WebGL conformance subtests now pass in `texture-npot.html`.
    # `immutable-tex-render-feedback.html` has more failures because some cases previously passed when a texture level failed to upload. They now reach the framebuffer completeness checks, which are still missing.
    # Most of the diff is updates to test expectations, the code change is small.

    # This exposes `FontFace`, `FontFaceSet`, `FontFaceSetLoadEvent` and `self.fonts` to workers. +https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/50049	(@rogerkorantenng, #50049)	fonts: Give workers their own font context (#50049)
    dom; FontFaceSet.status, FontFaceSetLoadEvent and the loading, loadingdone and loadingerror events, and FontFace and FontFaceSet in workers
    ^ commit 2c70a27b1d64225bb4480f06f33e9f221fb451f3
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Dedicated and shared workers used to share the page's `FontContext`. Fonts a worker loaded could end up in the page, and a font loading in a worker made the page's `document.fonts.ready` wait for it. Now each of these workers gets its own empty `FontContext`, which matches the spec ("Within a Worker document, the font source is initially empty") and what Firefox does. Fonts loaded with `self.fonts` are stored there, and their WebRender resources are freed when the worker shuts down, like the layout thread does for pages. Service workers already have their own `FontContext`, so they're unchanged.
    # 
    # This also fixes loading a second `FontFace` for a URL that was already loaded for the same family. That failed with "Failed to load font data" on pages too, because script loads skipped URLs that were already loaded.
    # 
    # Testing: a page's `document.fonts.ready` no longer waits for a slow font in a worker (7 ms instead of 2.7 s). With #49915 applied locally, a worker's canvas draws with the font the worker loaded, and can't see fonts loaded by the page. I also created and closed 20 workers that each load a font and draw text. Two page tests that loaded the same `FontFace` twice no longer error, and the WPT `workers` and canvas text tests still pass.
    # 
    # This builds on #50046, so its commits are included here until that one lands.
    # 
    # Part of #49892
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49941	(@mxmgorin, #49941)	pixels: Multiply only translucent pixels when premultiplying (#49941)
